<Accordion title="Generate video with supported providers">

[Video generation](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/video-generation) adds Kie AI, Z.AI, and Novita, with text and image workflows depending on the selected model. Qwen and Alibaba Wan can also animate local attachments and workspace images, with a matching image-to-video model selected for supported single-image requests. Kie's bundled plugin is enabled by default and requires an API key. Z.AI video uses its regional general API access and billing even when your chat setup uses a Coding Plan endpoint. Model-specific image, duration, resolution, and audio limits apply; the new integrations do not accept reference videos. Wan reference videos and Wan 2.6 reference-to-video images still need remote URLs, and the normal image intake limit remains 6 MB unless configured otherwise.

Sora has been removed following its upstream retirement, including the bundled custom OpenAI-compatible video endpoint route. Replace saved `openai/sora-2` or `openai/sora-2-pro` selections with another configured provider; these settings are not migrated automatically and can continue to fail.

- Show failed video and music providers in warning logs [#158914](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158914) — Thanks @ayushdevo, @obviyus, @kybrcore.

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Choose the right OpenAI sign-in and account">

OpenAI setup distinguishes Codex login, Sign in with ChatGPT (Beta), and API keys, with clearer account labels and model choices for the selected ChatGPT account. Setup puts Codex device-code and browser login first. Eligible ChatGPT sign-in requires registration and token-sharing consent; it does not grant every model or media capability. OpenClaw does not display its quota or per-app usage, or set per-app limits. Use the [authentication comparison](https://docs.openclaw.ai/providers/openai/authentication) to choose separate compatible credentials for media tools; ChatGPT-only onboarding keeps the agent's emoji instead of offering an unavailable generated avatar.

Verified same-account sign-in preserves existing conversation account choices, and covered account-removal paths clear deleted selections while keeping the chosen models. Older missing selections may still need an explicit account choice or reconnection. Using Sign in with ChatGPT (Beta) with native Codex supports automatic compaction, while manual compaction and remote execution remain outside that sign-in route. The [setup guide](https://docs.openclaw.ai/providers/openai/setup) explains those boundaries.

<Accordion title="Keep native Codex work and results connected">

The managed Codex runtime moves to 0.158.0 so successful command output remains available to completion events and OpenClaw history when streaming or polling races completion. Codex parents can also continue with worker results after yielding or ending their turn. Compaction and ordinary conversation forks retain more of the context they should carry, while canceled unsandboxed runs again clean up their native terminals.

These fixes require the updated runtime and do not automatically replay previously failed deliveries. Custom, remote, and desktop-owned app-servers retain the 0.149.0 minimum. [Model discovery](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/codex-harness-reference/model-discovery) now waits up to 10 seconds by default, so slow refreshes can finish; shorter configured timeouts still apply. Unused native history catalogs refresh on later demand, so silent changes may not appear until that refresh succeeds.

<Accordion title="Use native Codex tools and runtime options">

Eligible local Codex sessions can read complete saved documents when the inline preview is shortened, while granted optional plugin tools remain available and eligible tool declarations preserve nested argument details. Complete-file access requires unsandboxed local execution and the appropriate read permission. Owned local commands also honor configured preferred tool directories. You can opt into [Ultrafast selection](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/codex-harness/config-fields) with `appServer.enableUltrafast`; it is off by default and requests that tier only when the selected account and model advertise support, preserving normal tier selection otherwise.

For [user-home Codex connections](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/codex-harness/app-server), canonical OpenAI model choices now honor the provider configured in that native home. Existing chats can change route on a cold resume or fork. Check that provider supports your model; to require built-in OpenAI, set `model_provider = "openai"` in the native home and restart the app-server, or use an appropriately authenticated agent-home configuration.

<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Keep Claude CLI sessions and tools current">

Foreground Claude subagents can keep working beyond fifteen minutes when their activity shows meaningful progress, while silent work still faces the existing recovery limit. Manual compaction recognizes Claude CLI model aliases and preserves the bound conversation, including when a separate compaction model is configured. Follow-up shell tools also keep using the current policy after a plugin refresh, with approvals and denials still enforced.

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Preserve Anthropic cache and subscription settings">

[Anthropic requests](https://docs.openclaw.ai/providers/anthropic) retain the authentication and beta settings their selected route needs. Stored subscription credentials can use a newer installed Claude CLI identity, and eligible direct API-key requests keep required fallback headers alongside custom betas. Prompt caching also follows the provider's retained context, and rewinding avoids an unnecessary change to otherwise identical cached prompts.

<Accordion title="Preserve Copilot credentials and tool outcomes">

Copilot accounts from different tenants keep their separate credentials during Doctor repair, refresh, and logout. Agents that relied on cross-tenant sharing may need to sign in for their intended tenant. Copilot also preserves valid encrypted reasoning in newly saved history, and runs borrowing another agent's sandbox policy keep their own Code Mode controls without changing the underlying tool permissions.

<Accordion title="Browse model choices while discovery continues">

[Model pickers](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/models) keep the current choice easy to reach and preserve provider ordering from startup. Known choices stay available while discovery runs, and ordinary chat or status reads no longer start repeated refresh work that can hold up native agent runs. Reopen a chat model menu to see newly discovered choices. Role restrictions still remove unavailable choices promptly, while temporary refresh failures can retain eligible previously discovered models without promising those providers can currently serve them.

<Accordion title="Handle provider refusals and recoverable errors correctly">

Rejected provider requests are less likely to repeat simply because an error contains a misleading retry code or an empty body. Genuine temporary failures retain their existing recovery rules, and default-selected plugin background completions can use the agent's configured fallback models. Explicit model overrides remain strict. [Retry guidance](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/retry) explains the distinction; when a failed turn may already have used tools, check their results before trying again.

<Accordion title="Reduce repeated request and stream processing">

When a provider sends a burst of text, OpenClaw can combine fragments already waiting and avoid repeatedly processing earlier text. Buffered Anthropic replies also leave time for other conversations to progress. Preparing large eligible requests uses less of the thread serving other clients, though moving work or sharing that time can make an individual request take longer.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Improvements**

- Yield between buffered Anthropic stream events [#158410](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158410)
- Avoid full-prefix rescans in cumulative Responses streams [#158526](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158526)
- Combine queued text fragments before agent delivery [#158837](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158837)
- Skip unused extension dispatch during reply streaming [#158859](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158859)
- Move provider-request hashing off the Gateway thread [#159463](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159463)
- Encode eligible OpenAI-compatible HTTP requests once [#159574](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159574)

**Bug fixes**

- Decode CR-only Anthropic events and reduce repeated framing scans; mixed-ending support remains incomplete [#158503](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158503)

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Keep realtime voice context and playback consistent">

Gemini 3.8 Live preserves spoken user turns and handles agent consults with the settings each model supports. Extended Thinking stays active through interim speech and tool work, although continued silence after interruption remains best effort. Grok Talk saves one turn per settled utterance and supports longer buffered replies, while speaking over Talk replies works again during forced agent consults unless interruption is explicitly disabled.

<Accordion title="Use the optional Gemini Interactions route">

You can explicitly select the [Gemini Interactions route](https://docs.openclaw.ai/providers/google) for streamed replies, reasoning, images, and function tools. Existing Google defaults stay unchanged. This route sends the needed conversation history on each request with storage disabled, and does not support explicit cached-content handles or video clipping offsets. Interrupted replies can use the normal recovery policy, while malformed completed tool arguments remain rejected.

<Accordion title="Choose usable image and PDF model inputs">

[PDF analysis](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/pdf) selects credentials together with the model connection, fixing valid OpenAI accounts being rejected when Codex is enabled. If earlier choices do not resolve, an authenticated active vision model can serve as the final PDF fallback without duplicate setup. The image viewer can now detect and omit unreadable images while retaining valid ones in the same request. This prevents new corrupt-image failures without rewriting previously affected history.

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Read usage and choose supported reasoning effort">

Usage estimates follow the provider billing the request, while cumulative CLI token totals stay separate from current context usage. Missing SuperGrok quota percentages are explained instead of treated as zero. You can explicitly use [Ultra](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/thinking) for planning and verification guidance across OpenClaw and Claude Code models, with provider effort limited to what the model supports; native Codex requires usable reasoning choices. Ultra does not grant tools or add reasoning to a nonreasoning model, and cost estimates remain estimates rather than bills.

<Accordion title="Evaluate supplied evidence with a Decision model">

Agents with a configured [Decision model](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/decision-models) can explicitly ask Boolean, Choice, and Score questions through `decision_evaluate`. Only the supplied evidence goes to the selected local or hosted provider, with its resource use, limits, and charges; the result does not authorize an action. The default-off Decision assistance preference in Labs does not start automatic evaluations. Private or nightly TypeSafe configurations using the removed tool, skill, or model setting need to move to the shared Decision selection.

**Bug fixes**

- Preserve runtime context after provider review [efd9b981](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/efd9b9811e065d7a082ef8b8169dcdfd8fcf21ec)

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Keep ACP and native model choices separate">

ACP model choices now select the external harness separately from OpenClaw's own model calls. Review `agents.defaults.model` and its credentials after updating, because native calls can change provider, account, billing route, and data destination even when the ACP primary looked like a native model name. Signing in to the harness does not sign in to that native provider. The [model guide](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/models) explains the separation; active turns also retain their admitted account's capabilities through ordinary configuration reloads.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

- Keep active turns working through configuration reloads [#154462](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/154462) — Thanks @treylawrence, @vacinc, @oldrich333.
- Keep Gateway model commands out of local state preparation [#158136](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158136) — Thanks @vacinc.

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Continue supported Responses conversations after restart">

Applications using [Responses continuation](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/openresponses-http-api) can continue non-Incognito conversations across restarts. Mappings last up to 30 days, subject to the 5,000-entry limit and the session's own lifetime. An unresolved `previous_response_id` now returns HTTP 400 instead of silently starting a new conversation, even when a stable session key is supplied. Recover by resending the full history without that ID. Pre-update IDs disappear when the old Gateway exits; live Incognito continuation uses an authorized session key without a response ID.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Bug fixes**

- Avoid false aborted results for running async tools [#157212](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/157212) — Thanks @obviyus.

**Limits and compatibility**

- Persist response continuations and reject unresolved IDs [#159227](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159227)

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Connect Telnyx inference">

Telnyx users can install its official external provider with `openclaw plugins install telnyx` and connect an API key through the normal setup flow. The [Telnyx guide](https://docs.openclaw.ai/providers/telnyx) covers installation and model selection. Inference comes from the separately installed vendor plugin and depends on your account's access.

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Pass complete tool inputs through local Ollama">

Ollama-backed agents can avoid the missing-argument errors caused by incorrectly described free-form tool inputs. Native requests preserve those input definitions so affected Tool Search calls can pass their command and other arguments through to the tool. Normal argument validation remains.

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Replace the retired Fireworks default">

Fresh [Fireworks setups](https://docs.openclaw.ai/providers/fireworks) choose GLM 5.3 Fast in place of the retired GLM 5.2 Fast router. Existing explicit selections and aliases are preserved, so change a saved retired model yourself. The bundled cached-input price estimate changes from $0.21 to $0.39 per million tokens; the existing 256,000-token caps remain.

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Recognize current DeepSeek and Grok capabilities">

Qualifying standard Grok releases receive their supported thinking controls and image-input metadata without needing an exact model-name update. The rule excludes Grok 4.20 and variants such as Fast. Canonical DeepSeek Flash also honors thinking settings on affected OpenAI-compatible routes that do not use the provider's own wrapper. These changes recognize capabilities on supported routes without granting access to additional models.

</Accordion>

</AccordionGroup>

## Automations and Scheduling

Scheduled work is easier to configure and inspect, with clearer delivery outcomes and fixes for jobs that delegate work or encounter temporary failures.

<AccordionGroup>

<Accordion title="Configure schedules with valid timing and inputs">

The Automations editor catches invalid webhook addresses before saving, and recurring top-of-hour jobs retain the default window of up to five minutes when you turn off Exact timing and clear Stagger window. Clearing a saved window on an unchanged non-hourly schedule still switches it to exact timing. Lists also reuse more of their work, and searching an expanded list requires less browser work while results load. The [schedule guide](https://docs.openclaw.ai/automation/cron-jobs/schedules) explains the timing choices.

If your scripts create system-event jobs, remove `--timeout-seconds` from those commands. [Cron add and create](https://docs.openclaw.ai/cli/cron) now reject that previously ignored option. Agent and command jobs keep their timeout support, while script jobs use `--script-timeout-seconds`. Invalid timezone errors now point to `--tz` so you can correct the right input.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Improvements**

- Reuse unchanged automation list results and look up pending-message names in the selected store [#158077](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158077).
- Reduce loading-related browser style work when searching expanded Automations lists [#159901](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159901). Thanks @vyctorbrzezowski.

**Bug fixes**

- Reject malformed webhook URLs and embedded credentials in the editor before saving [#146706](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/146706). Thanks @pengzh1, @obviyus, and @aniruddhaadak80.
- Preserve hourly default staggering when saving and reopening timing choices [#156974](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/156974).
- Let agents read automation jobs and update results containing scheduler error counts [#157497](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/157497). Thanks @he-yufeng, @obviyus, @nicolasderito, and @xiaozishan.
- Identify invalid timezones separately from invalid timestamps and nonexistent daylight-saving times [#159756](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159756).

**Limits and compatibility**

- Reject unsupported timeout flags on system-event creation without changing stored jobs [#159716](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159716).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Choose recipients and understand delivery outcomes">

When setting up an announcement, select a channel and enter its Account ID to see known conversation targets in the To field. Suggestions leave the account and topic under your control, and you can still enter a destination manually. Scheduled announcements receive their channel's formatting instructions, and successfully sent command or script announcements with explicit destinations can enter the conversation history so the agent has context for your reply.

[Webhook delivery](https://docs.openclaw.ai/automation/cron-jobs/delivery) now stays Unknown when the receiver may have received the request but no response confirms it. OpenClaw does not retry those ambiguous sends, and the CLI shows `delivery unknown` separately from successful execution. Required delivery keeps completion unknown; best-effort delivery can finish without claiming receipt.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Improvements**

- Suggest account-scoped announcement targets, preserve manual entry, and clear stale topics after routing changes; failure-alert suggestions remain separate [05af7bc](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/05af7bc4b306987f15b83092d4c8b366dc8f194b). Thanks @zeroaltitude.

**Bug fixes**

- Record successful explicit-destination command and script announcements in chat context when destination lookup and session admission succeed [#156962](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/156962). Thanks @obviyus and @fourestfire.
- Supply scheduled chat announcements with the delivering channel and account's formatting rules [#157877](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/157877). Thanks @obviyus.
- Accept disabled failure alerts through the automations tool while keeping auto-disable safety notices; restricted-schema providers retain object-only settings [#158325](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158325). Thanks @obviyus and @joshpetras.
- Preserve unknown webhook delivery after timeouts, interrupted responses, and ambiguous redirects without replaying the send [#159840](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159840). Thanks @joshavant.

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Read the retained transcript for an automation run">

Choose [View transcript](https://docs.openclaw.ai/automation/cron-jobs/managing-jobs) on an automation run to read its recorded conversation and load earlier messages. Cron history and transcripts remain available after Tasks retirement. Isolated runs open their recorded conversation, while custom-session jobs show the conversation shared across runs. Switching automations closes the previous viewer, and unavailable history offers retry guidance. Cleanup rechecks whether history still qualifies for deletion; retention limits remain unchanged, and previously deleted history is not restored. The [sessions CLI](https://docs.openclaw.ai/cli/sessions) can also archive or delete a stored cron-run session by its exact key.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Improvements**

- Give conversation-history reads a separate queue from cron retention scans, with an extra on-demand reader and memory and cold-start tradeoffs [#155998](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/155998).
- Move scheduled-job history reads off the Gateway thread while preserving filters, pagination, and current access checks [#158222](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158222).
- Open and page the recorded automation transcript directly through Cron, preserving the selected session generation and checking access on every page [#158776](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158776).

**Bug fixes**

- Resolve exact cron-run session keys for CLI deletion, archiving, and dry runs while retaining confirmation and identity checks [#158212](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158212). Thanks @obviyus and @abacha.
- Recheck cron history grouping and current rank before overflow deletion; this change's managed-flow cancellation repair preceded the later TaskFlow removal [#159064](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159064).
- Close old transcripts when switching automation panels and ignore their delayed replies while retaining same-panel selections [#159160](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159160).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Keep scheduled jobs moving after transient failures">

A temporary database error while catching up overdue jobs at startup no longer leaves future jobs unscheduled after the fault clears, and the original error remains visible. Scheduled runs using supported local Ollama or OpenAI-compatible endpoints also [check the provider again on their next run](https://docs.openclaw.ai/automation/cron-jobs/payloads) after a probe timeout. The timed-out run can still be skipped. Run reservations now wait for database access away from the Gateway thread, allowing other work to continue while the reservation is pending.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Improvements**

- Avoid redundant database snapshots during cron receipt checks without changing admission or deletion checks [#156801](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/156801).

**Bug fixes**

- Preserve command output, exit status, and timeout details when cleanup fails; uncertain cleanup still reports an error and keeps backup write ownership held [c6709b4](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/c6709b460e637f7c0c5cf6eda4c97a17c6fc5933). Thanks @masatohoshino.
- Stop caching client-side local-provider probe timeouts for five minutes, allowing the next isolated run to probe again [#156237](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/156237). Thanks @obviyus and @charlie-morrison.
- Resume future scheduling after transient startup catch-up failure, including subsequent suspension and resume [#159086](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159086). Thanks @obviyus and @yetval.
- Move manual, scheduled, and startup catch-up reservations to the database worker; execution still waits for a durable reservation [#159792](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159792).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Keep delegated automation within its existing authority">

Scheduled work can launch visible workers with the execution permissions already approved for the parent turn, without an erroneous administrator-permission request. Configured channel owners can also resume automation management after delegated work finishes. These fixes preserve narrower permissions and existing tool restrictions; explicitly requesting Full Access still requires administrator authority. Scheduled jobs now read child progress and answers in the background so those checks leave the Gateway thread available for other work.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Bug fixes**

- Recognize existing one-use automation management grants after subagent completion while retaining handler validation [#157404](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/157404). Thanks @obviyus.
- Preserve the parent's effective execution mode for newly spawned workers without granting administrator identity or rewriting existing sessions [#158270](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158270). Thanks @fuller-stack-dev and Jason Sy.
- Read scheduled-job child status and answers through the database worker while retaining successor, paused-work, queued-delivery, and cancellation handling [#160508](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160508).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Keep heartbeat notices and shutdown status accurate">

A completed [heartbeat](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/heartbeat) that decides no notification is needed now suppresses generated tool-failure warnings and attachments too. Later runner failures retain their alerts. Doctor describes disabled heartbeat monitors as disabled, and restarting no longer hangs on a queued heartbeat job waiting for shutdown. That waiting job is marked skipped when its handler becomes unavailable.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Bug fixes**

- Settle queued heartbeat jobs as skipped during shutdown to avoid a circular restart wait [24babfe](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/24babfe94446197e4d9dd35dac8a14556943d7f5). Credit Peter Steinberger.
- Honor completed quiet heartbeat decisions and suppress their exact unsent warning records; already queued or ambiguous sends keep their recovery handling [#156174](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/156174). Thanks @pash-openai.
- Correct Doctor's create and update summaries for disabled monitors without changing their stored enabled state [#159711](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159711).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Run automations from completed Workboard work">

Enabled automations attached to a [Workboard](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/workboard) can run when linked workers finish, even after the worker's own tool permissions have ended. Repeated completion events for a board remain combined within a 60-second window, disabled jobs stay excluded, and the normal schedule remains a backstop. Older hosts without the required scheduler operation report that the nudge is unavailable.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Bug fixes**

- Let the active Workboard service request completion-triggered runs while retaining disabled-job, cron-origin, and stopped-service exclusions [#156130](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/156130). Thanks @joshavant.

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Renew Gmail watches within their lifecycle">

[Gmail watch renewal](https://docs.openclaw.ai/automation/cron-jobs/gmail) keeps its existing cadence, runs once after an overdue wake, and avoids overlapping renewals. Stopping or replacing the watcher cancels and waits for its in-flight renewal before retiring it, with the existing forwarding behavior and configuration unchanged.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Improvements**

- Coordinate Gmail watch renewal with scheduling, replacement, and shutdown in the Gateway and standalone Gmail service [#158913](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158913).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Reduce interference from scheduled-session maintenance">

Cleanup of old scheduled sessions moves large scans away from the Gateway thread and avoids copying saved session snapshots a second time. This reduces work that can interfere with other activity while preserving retention rules, active-work protection, and transcript archiving. Cleanup can still take longer overall in custom stores. The [scheduling guide](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/architecture#timed-work-and-shutdown) also explains late wakes, startup catch-up, and the work shutdown waits for.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Improvements**

- Discover expired cron-run sessions in a worker while retaining shared-store ownership and deletion safeguards [#155414](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/155414).
- Reduce temporary allocation during cleanup and previews without making previews persistent or changing retention policy [#160510](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160510).

**Documentation**

- Explain coalesced late timer wakes, Cron's own execution and catch-up policy, and shutdown waiting for registered work [#160477](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160477).

</details>

</Accordion>

</AccordionGroup>

## Browser and Computer Use

Browser and desktop work is easier to follow from the conversation, with more ways to interact with the agent's page and continue after a manual step. The changes also address setup failures and cleanup when computer work ends.

<AccordionGroup>

<Accordion title="Use Lightpanda for page text and forms">

You can opt into experimental [Lightpanda browser profiles](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/browser/lightweight) for reading pages and filling forms. You must start Lightpanda separately and connect an attach-only profile to a dedicated, protected WebSocket endpoint. Each connection has one temporary page, and a disconnect requires reopening it and taking a fresh snapshot. Chromium remains the default and the choice for screenshots, PDFs, file transfers, persistent sign-ins, multiple tabs, and sites Lightpanda cannot handle.

The setup guide includes pinned installation examples and optional comparison tools. Review its licensing guidance before deployment or redistribution, since Lightpanda 0.4.1 uses AGPL-3.0-or-later while OpenClaw's adapter remains MIT-licensed.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Improvements**

- Add explicitly selected, externally started Lightpanda profiles with capability checks and connection-owned pages. [b7eb012](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/b7eb012f643b24b561fe67d974c1b643788114b5) — @vincentkoc
- Show supported browser engines and their session and screenshot capabilities in status, without implying an engine is installed or running. [0a5178c](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/0a5178c483b5aecfc4a27ecffccf69323c2d52fd) — @vincentkoc
- Add an optional Chromium headless-shell benchmark selection with distinct result labels; this does not change the production browser or establish resource savings. [f5cf1b3](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/f5cf1b31e1fc988b1dc225cb6152f2d683ee9e2e) — @vincentkoc

**Documentation**

- Add pinned native Linux/macOS and Linux-container setup examples, plus an opt-in text-and-form benchmark; Windows guidance uses containers or WSL. [645f1bc](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/645f1bc4ef49f9056b54d39b9c4ab7219776c1b3) — @vincentkoc
- Document engine licensing, artifact provenance, and redistribution obligations in the September 21 audit, without treating checksums as permission to redistribute. [322e358](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/322e358bb0ca00ce92ebe2ddc8ce9d23f652c8f1) — @vincentkoc

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Return to the browser tab for the conversation">

The chat [Browser panel](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/browser/profiles) shows tabs belonging to or referenced by that conversation. New tabs opened there follow session cleanup and close on reset or deletion; older untracked tabs stay open in the Browser dock. On [Android](https://docs.openclaw.ai/platforms/android#agent-browser-in-chat), an Agent browser card lets you interact with the same remote tab without leaving chat or losing your draft. It requires administrator permission and an updated Gateway with the bundled Control UI enabled. Closing the card hides it without closing the tab.

Eligible conversation writers can also use [Session browser dashboards](https://docs.openclaw.ai/web/dashboards) without global browser administration. These start with empty cookies and require Playwright plus the default local managed Chromium profile. Own-session permissions remain limited to your sessions, and read-only, sandbox-required, or locked-model sessions are excluded. Dashboard page state is lost on Stop, reset, or restart. Separately, shared views refit after remote resizing, and browser cards select the intended page after delayed rendering.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Improvements**

- Limit chat Browser tab listings to session-owned and conversation-referenced tabs, with session cleanup for newly panel-opened tabs and the full Browser dock still available. [#159891](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159891)
- Add Android in-chat remote browser control, touch and keyboard input, collapse, and reopening under existing administrator access. [#159767](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159767) — @iwhatsskill, @ivankuznetsov
- Let eligible session writers interact with isolated managed-Chromium dashboards, separate from administrator browsing; remote, attached, personal, node, and Lightpanda profiles are unsupported, and dashboard records remain in memory with a 64-record limit. [#156371](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/156371) — @vincentkoc

**Bug fixes**

- Open the browser card's selected page after the panel finishes delayed rendering. [#153253](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/153253) — @vyctorbrzezowski
- Give browser streaming one startup window of up to 30 seconds before cancelling a stalled attempt and falling back to a screenshot, followed by the existing retry. [#156323](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/156323) — @vincentkoc
- Keep ordinary Chrome tabs usable when Chrome Web Store or another recognized protected page refuses control; the protected page stays excluded and no extension update is required. [#156890](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/156890) — @holny, @obviyus, @liyoulu
- Refit visible shared browser views after remote resizing and discard late native-browser errors from a previous tab or session. [#157087](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/157087)

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Hand desktop control between the agent and a person">

After you sign in or finish another manual step on a [conversation-attached or cloud-session desktop](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/cloud-workers/desktop), the agent can explicitly take control again using its existing permission, without a separate viewer handoff token. The viewer returns to view-only, the agent takes a fresh screenshot, and interrupted input is not replayed. You can reclaim control at any time. This handoff applies to attached and cloud-session desktops, not ordinary Gateway or paired-node desktops.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Improvements**

- Add explicit agent takeover after manual control, invalidate old observations, preserve human reclaim, and show a neutral reconnect message after a clean viewer disconnect. [#159923](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159923)
- Update macOS desktop automation to Peekaboo 4.6.0 for capture and input fixes; contended actions time out if they cannot start within 15 seconds, while already executing actions keep their locks. [#158022](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158022)

**Bug fixes**

- Treat blank optional computer selectors as omitted while retaining errors for invalid or unknown nonblank selections. [#155072](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/155072) — @ceckert, @obviyus
- When tool-loop detection is enabled, recognize repeated reads of an unchanged window despite fresh element references and apply the existing no-progress stop. [#160019](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160019) — @obviyus, @grape404

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Listen to managed Linux desktops">

You can explicitly unmute application sound in embedded, docked, and standalone [managed Linux desktop views](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/config-browser-ui-desktop), including when you have view-only screen access. Connections start muted, and sound stops when you hide the panel or browser tab, disconnect, or lose access. Reconnecting starts muted again. Listening carries application output only, without microphone forwarding or guaranteed audio/video synchronization, and uses about 1.5 Mbit/s. Audio capture is limited to managed Linux Gateway desktops.

Install `pulseaudio` and `pulseaudio-utils` and restart the managed desktop if audio is unavailable. Successful setup redirects its applications to a private output, replacing any inherited PulseAudio route. If initial setup fails, the existing route is preserved and is not captured. The Audio unavailable control reveals setup guidance on hover, focus, or tap.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Improvements**

- Add optional audio availability information to desktop observations while preserving screen-only clients. [#157718](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/157718) — @vincentkoc
- Capture a private managed-Linux application output and recover audio independently of healthy desktop applications; exhausted recovery leaves audio disabled with restart guidance. [#157720](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/157720) — @vincentkoc
- Report the current audio source after recovery, with setup guidance and sanitized failure information. [#157723](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/157723) — @vincentkoc
- Add explicit Unmute and Mute controls to embedded and docked desktop panels, with stop-on-hide and muted reconnect behavior. [#157724](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/157724) — @vincentkoc
- Add the same audio controls to the standalone Desktop view and keep its toolbar usable on narrow screens. [#157726](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/157726) — @vincentkoc
- Move optional audio setup guidance into the Audio unavailable control while retaining playback-error alerts. [#159487](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159487)

**Security and trust**

- Require screen authentication and explicit start before audio capture, using expiring one-use bearer tickets, and end audio with its screen observation. [#157722](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/157722) — @vincentkoc

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Keep Codex Computer Use compatible with Desktop changes">

Mac users whose [Codex Computer Use](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/codex-computer-use) stopped working after a Desktop update can start it again with the replacement plugin and newer app layouts. Restart the Gateway after updating OpenClaw to load the repair. macOS permissions and explicit native server or tool restrictions still apply, so custom legacy policies may need a manual update.

An explicitly disabled legacy Computer Use plugin now blocks automatic replacement, even if the replacement is already installed. An owner or authorized administrator can run `/codex computer-use install` explicitly without clearing that disable. To restore automatic readiness, re-enable the legacy native setting too.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Bug fixes**

- Prepare the replacement macOS Computer Use runtime and refresh changed desktop assets and sibling plugins while respecting custom identities and native policy. [#156556](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/156556) — @bdjben, @shakkernerd
- Recognize newer signed Desktop executable layouts and create writable isolated launch configuration without changing the signed app or its permissions. [#158480](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158480) — @jalehman
- Honor an explicit native disable during automatic setup, preserve explicit owner/admin installation, and fix symlinked cache discovery and stale launchers. [#160133](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160133) — @bdjben

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Use supported browser and desktop helpers under Bun">

Bun installations can run the packaged Chrome connector and managed-desktop control helper using OpenClaw's own runtime, without a separate Node installation for those helpers. The packaged Chrome connector also stops attempting an unnecessary npm update check. Custom browser launch commands remain under your control, and Windows extension native messaging still requires `node.exe`.

[Terminal support under Bun](https://docs.openclaw.ai/install/bun-compatibility#known-limitations) has a narrower requirement. Node-free terminals on macOS and Linux need a compatible Bun build with the required flow-control and exit fixes; stock Bun 1.4.2 still needs Node, and this change does not update app runtime versions. Bun builds without the separate inherited-stream fix can also take about 30 seconds to close a desktop execution that launched an app.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Bug fixes**

- Launch packaged Chrome MCP with OpenClaw's current runtime on the next browser session, preserving custom commands. [#159422](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159422)
- Avoid the packaged Chrome server's npm startup update probe while retaining custom servers' existing checks. [#160226](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160226)
- Run managed desktop control with the Gateway's executable, removing that helper's separate Node requirement. [#159462](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159462)

**Limits and compatibility**

- Enable native terminals on compatible macOS/Linux Bun builds, retaining the Node helper for stock Bun and node-pty for Windows and Node runtimes. [#159447](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159447)

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Browser and desktop cleanup">

Cancelling a run during attached-desktop setup, or rejecting a computer action, no longer leaves those prepared desktops outside end-of-run cleanup. Cleanup failures remain visible. Browser activity also avoids pauses caused by writing tab records to a busy database. [Node shutdown](https://docs.openclaw.ai/nodes/node-host) now waits for asynchronous cleanup and reports failures; a plugin that never finishes cleaning up can still leave graceful shutdown or an automatic node update waiting indefinitely.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Bug fixes**

- Await node and desktop-worker cleanup before reporting shutdown complete, and require reconnecting after failed plugin disconnect cleanup. [#156995](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/156995)
- Retain responsibility for releasing an attached desktop when setup is cancelled, without sending the cancelled input. [#157997](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/157997) — @vincentkoc
- Release every attached-desktop preparation at run end after rejected actions, including preparations that were never selected for use. [#158546](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158546) — @vincentkoc
- Move an in-root Browser symlink to Trash without moving its outside or missing destination, while continuing to refuse escaped parent directories. [#158640](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158640)
- Move Browser tab database work off the main thread while preserving tab ownership, retention, and completion of accepted work during shutdown. [#159599](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159599)

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Connect the intended browser and search account">

Automatic [web search](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/web) now tries usable xAI OAuth before a Tavily environment key, falling back to Tavily if Grok fails. Explicit provider choices keep their existing behavior. Browser connections also retain authorization supplied in standard header formats, and Windows browser detection handles install paths with spaces without printing PowerShell probe errors into Doctor diagnostics.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Bug fixes**

- Restore OAuth-aware Grok-before-Tavily automatic search selection while preserving explicit provider pins and their no-fallback behavior. [#156048](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/156048) — @holny, @obviyus, @darkjuanfra
- Preserve CDP authorization supplied as Headers objects or header tuples, including explicit-header precedence over URL credentials. [#159430](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159430)
- Detect Windows browser versions in paths with spaces and keep failed probes quiet, retaining the existing conditional directory fallback. [#160432](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160432) — @irish-joseph, @obviyus, @avirtudazo-officeconnect, @ayushdevo

</details>

</Accordion>

</AccordionGroup>

## Plugins and Integrations

Plugin pages explain more of what an integration offers before you enable it, while installation, reloads, and connected agents receive fixes for interrupted work and changing settings.

<AccordionGroup>

<Accordion title="Start and stop cloud sessions without blocking unrelated work">

Stopping one cloud session no longer holds unrelated starts behind another provision, and an interrupted Stop can resume saving workspace edits after a restart or update while the original machine remains available. Bootstrap downloads can retry and resume interrupted transfers, and compatible [warm images](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/cloud-workers/warm-images) avoid an unnecessary replacement snapshot before starting. [Cloud-worker recovery](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/cloud-workers/session-lifecycle) still needs the retained machine and successful saving and cleanup. A lost machine leaves only the last accepted workspace, forced destruction discards edits, and failed cleanup can leave a billable machine. Automatic cleanup parks after ten attempts or one hour; inspect and stop the Crabbox lease, then retry Stop in the conversation.

Every Crabbox provider now requires **0.67.0 or newer**. Supported local binaries work offline, and connected hosts can use a verified managed download. If downloads are restricted, stage a supported executable before updating or even inspection and cleanup can fail. Existing binaries and profiles are preserved, but the old 0.56.0 fallback is removed. Affected Azure, Daytona, and GCP operators should review the [Crabbox 0.67.0 migration notes](https://github.com/openclaw/crabbox/releases/tag/v0.67.0) for ephemeral disks, organization identity, and kept-lease expiry.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Improvements**

- Defer replacement snapshots for compatible changed-commit warm starts; disabling reserves also disables automatic image refresh [#156380](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/156380)
- Reduce remote snapshot-preparation commands and allow slower configured CLI probes [#156767](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/156767)
- Prepare first-dispatch runtime and worker archives concurrently [#159520](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159520)
- Negotiate completion waits with paired nodes while older nodes retain polling [#160162](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160162)
- Reduce paired POSIX host startup loading; update and restart the node host to use it [#160163](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160163)
- Avoid full file application when both remote and local workspaces remain unchanged [#160231](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160231)

**Bug fixes**

- Keep active worker history and Stop requests tied to their original session store [#156452](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/156452)
- Preserve unfinished recovery work when its selected session store or authority changes [#156543](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/156543)
- Resume worker event delivery from saved acknowledgments and reject stale completion replay [#156612](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/156612)
- Bound failed environment cleanup retries while retaining unconfirmed, potentially billed leases [#158867](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158867)
- Allow coordinator inspection retries more time within provisioning deadlines [#159399](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159399)
- Release unrelated cloud starts after Stop or cancellation of an unstarted Move [#159482](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159482)
- Resume workspace saving for an interrupted Stop across restarts and updates [#159484](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159484)
- Retry temporary bootstrap download failures within existing authorization limits [#159546](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159546)
- Resume partial Crabbox bootstrap downloads with final size and hash verification; separate node-host bundle downloads remain unchanged [#160655](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160655)

**Documentation**

- Correct profile reload guidance; new provisioning uses edited settings while running workers retain their settings, and reload-off mode retains its restart requirements [#159640](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159640)

- Raise the minimum to Crabbox 0.67.0 for every cloud-worker provider and remote-proof backend [#160560](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160560)

</details>
</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Run application previews on their assigned worker">

Conversation writers can open interactive [application previews](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/portals) on a dedicated attached secondary worker when their session and tool permissions allow it. Sandbox-required and model-locked sessions are excluded. Preview URLs are bearer links, so anyone holding one can use it while it remains active, even after the initiating person loses access. Close the preview to withdraw its link. For foreground apps on eligible Linux leases, [Crabbox commands](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/secrets/secret-store-and-egress#model-credentials-for-crabbox-commands) can also use host-held model API keys without copying the real key remotely. This requires a supported API-key route and proxy-aware client; OAuth and detached apps are unsupported. Credential revocation alone does not prove the remote process stopped.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Improvements**

- Run prepared foreground Crabbox applications with command-scoped host credentials; source and dependencies must already be present, and the grant does not contain network access or restrict model operations [#156207](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/156207)
- Open, list, and close previews on the conversation's dedicated attached worker; reconnect or update an unavailable node before retrying [#156373](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/156373) — @vincentkoc

</details>
</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Find and configure plugins by their capabilities">

Plugin details now show declared capabilities, setup guides, and the expected inputs of available tools, making it easier to understand an integration before using it. Search displays available artwork and opens the matching catalog page, while enabled media-generating providers can appear under Media as well as Models. Capabilities describe what a plugin offers, not whether your account is connected or permission has been granted. [Customize UI](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/feature-plugins) moves into inline Advanced settings, with `/settings/plugins?tab=advanced` available for returning to the built-in interface. Curated ordering and the Computer use category depend on the coordinated ClawHub catalog service being available.

<Accordion title="Install and recover the intended plugin package">

`openclaw plugins install <package> --no-enable` installs a package while preserving your activation choices, including an already-enabled plugin. Bun-only installations can now manage npm plugins without a separate Node or npm installation. Installation failures distinguish **Retry install** from **View status**, so an uncertain result sends you to inspect the installation before trying again. Native plugin loading also preserves companion libraries and recovers from specific missing-capture and Doctor inspection failures.

[Plugin updates and replacement installs](https://docs.openclaw.ai/cli/plugins/uninstall-and-update) now apply supported plugin-owned configuration repairs before activation. Data migrations still require `openclaw doctor --fix`, and disabled plugins keep pending work. Automatic repair does not cover every Doctor contract, so check migrated listener settings after updating. An older updater that rejects legacy settings before loading the new code needs the core-update-first sequence. If rollback cannot be confirmed, the matching package is retained with a visible failure.

<Accordion title="Reload or remove plugins while work is active">

[Plugin reloads](https://docs.openclaw.ai/cli/plugins/uninstall-and-update) can wait for existing work to finish, and `openclaw plugins reload <ids...> --wait` extends that wait beyond the normal 60-second limit. Run it outside a turn holding the target plugin. Cancelling before replacement is published lets the old version resume after successful rollback; cancelling afterward does not undo the replacement. Reload still neither rebuilds source nor restarts OpenClaw, and changed compiled bundled code can require a restart. Browser-only rebuilds use **Reload plugin UI** without unnecessarily replacing an unchanged backend.

Removal also rechecks whether settings still reference the plugin's files. If a reference returns, remove it before retrying. If deletion has already begun when ownership changes, further deletion stops and the plugin stays disabled and tracked for retry; files already removed are not restored.

<Accordion title="Preserve the right files during plugin cleanup">

Loaded native plugin files remain retained until the process exits, avoiding repeated attempts to delete libraries still in use. A later startup can reclaim eligible retired captures, while loaded or referenced files stay retained. Cleanup of aged legacy scratch remains best-effort and can disrupt an older producer when process visibility is unavailable. Plugin authors also need private, user-owned temporary roots without group or world write access; replace shared roots such as `/tmp` with the existing secure-root resolver.

[LanceDB statistics](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/memory-lancedb) now use an isolated reader with a 60-second database-read deadline and return zero without creating a missing memory table. That deadline starts after plugin registration, so it does not bound the entire command's startup time.

- Enforce existing Team Reports and Google Chat file limits while files are being read [#158181](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158181)

**Limits and compatibility**

</details>
</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Discover MCP tools and release their connections">

[MCP discovery](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/mcp) gives a server without an explicit timeout ten seconds to list its tools, up from 1.5 seconds, so slower catalogs can reach new chats and scheduled runs. An unresponsive server can therefore delay listing longer; explicit timeouts still take precedence. Repeated startup failures now back off and show retry guidance, while session and command cleanup respects connections still in use. Saving OAuth credentials does not reset an existing cooldown, so newly authorized tools may remain unavailable until its deadline, up to ten minutes, or an explicit MCP reload in the owning process.

- Wait for an already-closing idle connection during session reset or deletion [#157041](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/157041)

<Accordion title="Keep ACP sessions usable through repair and cancellation">

ACP agents accept prompts again after an in-process Gateway restart, and ordinary file-backed session operations move database work off the main thread. [Stop](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/acp-agents/runbook) waits for accepted work and started cleanup to settle, though replacement-session protection remains incomplete across manual cancel, fast abort, and Gateway reset paths. Codex and Claude launchers can fall back to their pinned adapter packages after captured files disappear. Restart the Gateway after updating to regenerate old launchers; an uncached fallback may need registry access.

[ACPX storage](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/acp-agents/quickstart) now defaults to the writable Gateway state directory. Legacy adoption runs only when the destination is absent or empty. Existing adapter or authentication files can prevent adoption and leave old sessions out of view, even though their files remain. Check your sessions after updating and retain the old location through the existing `stateDir` override when needed.

- Move file-backed ACP listings to database workers while preserving detached metadata [#158782](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158782)

<Accordion title="Track OpenCode sessions and activity">

[OpenCode v2 sessions](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/reference/opencode) are visible again for browsing, transcript reading, and activity tracking, while v1 remains supported. Empty sessions now retain a valid activity baseline for detecting later work outside OpenClaw. Listings look beyond archived pages within their scan budget and report an error if that budget is exhausted rather than presenting an empty catalog.

<Accordion title="Keep meeting captions and transcripts attributable">

Meeting captions and saved transcripts can retain where an observation came from and its speaker label, making the captured conversation easier to interpret. Labels remain descriptive, not verified identities or a complete edit history. The experimental Google Meet participation interface reports available capabilities, but OpenClaw's Google Meet adapter currently exposes no native chat, reaction, or hand actions. Transcript saving and lookup also reduce repeated storage work, and partial audio startup failures clean up the output process before reporting the original error.

<Accordion title="Preserve Team Reports while reducing main-thread work">

[Team Reports](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/team-reports) keeps accepted daily reports, counts, and overlapping weekly or monthly reports when GitHub or Discord collection fails. Check the status warnings and regenerate the affected day after access recovers; a first failed collection leaves that period unpublished. Collection and aggregation now run in workers to reduce main-thread pauses. Startup catch-up does not check every changed source setting, so manually regenerate affected days after changing Discord channels or collection filters.

<Accordion title="Build plugins against supported host capabilities">

Plugin authors gain shared [workspace-context helpers](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/sdk-entrypoints) and provider utilities for model aliases, streamed messages, and assistant replies. Plugins using new exports must declare a compatible host through `openclaw.compat.pluginApi`. Existing completion APIs now preserve useful cancellation and permission errors, and agent tools can reach services already started by their own Gateway. Visitor tools also return usable lists and invitation results in Code Mode. Revoke existing visitor grants before retargeting the configured account, application, or policy.
